Ignore.\n\nNow, task: parallelize cholesky with OpenMP. Need output matching original semantics (results hash). Existing algorithm: row-major, standard right-looking? Actually it's a row-oriented left-looking-ish algorithm: for i, for j<=i, compute dot product of rows i and j; if diagonal take sqrt. Then zero upper triangle.\n\nWe can parallelize directly the j loop for a given i, but diagonal dependencies within the row j loop. For row i, elements j <= i: off-diagonal j compute uses A[j][j] (already computed for j < i) and A[i][k], A[j][k] for k<j. Those are already computed portions of row i (indices k<j) — yes, for row i, A[i][k] for k<j is computed earlier in the j loop. So parallelizing over j within same i is not straightforward because each j depends on earlier k of row i (the dot product uses A[i*n+k] for k<j).
